`RELEASING.md`:

```md
# Releasing ACF

## Channels

| Channel | What it is | How it is made | `ACF.Channel` |
|---------|------------|----------------|---------------|
| development | Every commit on `main` | `tools/verify.sh` must print `VERIFY OK` before each commit | `"development"` |
| canary | A release candidate, run in real servers before anyone relies on it | `tools/release.sh canary` → tag `v<VERSION>-rc.<N>` | `"canary"` |
| stable | A canary that met the promotion criteria, re-labelled, not rebuilt from new code | `tools/release.sh stable` → tag `v<VERSION>` | `"stable"` |

The repository always says `development`. The release script stamps the channel and
release version, for example `1.0.0-rc.1`, into a staged copy of `src/`. A running server
reports it in `ACF.Version`, `ACF.Channel` and `ACF:GetStatus()`, so audit entries and
logs can be attributed to a build.

## Making a release

```bash
tools/release.sh canary     # v1.0.0-rc.1, v1.0.0-rc.2, ...
git push origin v1.0.0-rc.1
```

The script refuses to run unless:

- the working tree is clean;
- `tools/verify.sh` passes (tests, strict type check, build);
- `CHANGELOG.md` has a `## [<VERSION>]` entry.

It writes these files to `build/release/`:

| File | Contents |
|------|----------|
| `ACF-<release>.rbxl` | A place with ACF installed |
| `ACF-<release>.rbxm` | A model with three folders. Drag their contents into ServerScriptService, ReplicatedStorage and StarterPlayerScripts. |
| `ACF-<release>.json` | Manifest: version, channel, commit, test result, SHA-256 checksums |

```bash
tools/release.sh stable     # only on a commit already tagged v<VERSION>-rc.N
git push origin v1.0.0
```

**Stable is never new code.** The script requires the exact commit to carry a canary tag.

## Running a canary in Roblox

Roblox runs one copy of your code in every server, so a canary is the new build
**published everywhere with enforcement held back**. `examples/CanaryBootstrap.server.luau`
shows how:

1. Publish with automatic enforcement in shadow mode on every server
   (`ENFORCE_PERCENT = 0`). Detection, risk and enforcement *decisions* all run; nothing
   automatic is applied. Staff actions still work.
2. Ship the audit log (`ACF:OnEnforcementAudit`) to your own storage. Watch it against
   the criteria below.
3. Raise `ENFORCE_PERCENT` (10 → 50 → 100). Servers are chosen by a hash of `JobId`.
4. Promote the commit to stable.

**Rollback** is publishing the previous stable build. Ban records and risk history use
versioned DataStore records (`Version = 1`) that older builds read unchanged.

## Promotion criteria (canary → stable)

These must hold over at least 3 days and 500 player-hours of real traffic:

| Criterion | Where to see it |
|-----------|-----------------|
| No automatic removal (`ShadowAction` Kick/Ban) of a player staff judge honest | Audit log, reviewed by staff |
| Restrictions (`Rejected`) on honest players are rare and explained | Audit log; `Rejected` entries with `Tags` |
| No framework errors: no `Failed` modules, no disabled scheduled tasks except your own | `ACF:GetStatus()` (Modules, Scheduler), logs |
| Per-tick cost within budget (target < 2 ms at your player cap) | `GetStatus().Scheduler[...].MaxCpuSeconds` |
| DataStore writes succeed (`PersistenceFailed` absent, or explained by an outage) | Audit log, logs |
| The offline evaluation is unchanged: no honest evidence, every cheat caught | `tools/evaluate.sh full`, [docs/EVALUATION.md](docs/EVALUATION.md) |

If any criterion fails, fix it on `main` and cut a new canary (`rc.N+1`). Never promote
a canary that needed a fix.

## Version numbers

- **Semantic Versioning.** Major for breaking API, configuration or record-format changes.
  Minor for features. Patch for fixes.
- **Configuration.** Changing a configuration *default* that alters enforcement is at
  least a minor version, and the CHANGELOG says so.
- **Stored records.** Record formats carry `Version`. A new format must read the old one.

```

`ROADMAP.md`:

```md
# ACF Roadmap

Phases run in order. A phase starts only after the previous phase's acceptance criteria
are verified.

**Studio status (2026-11-17):** the full suite (278/278) and live Play-mode scenarios pass
in Roblox Studio, including R6/R15 rigs, seats, ladders, terrain water, moving platforms
and accessory hits. See TESTING.md, "Studio verification log". A 50-character NPC load test and character-morph tests also pass (2026-11-19). The only
remaining check is network-level load with real clients (Phase 8).

| Phase | Version | Status |
|-------|---------|--------|
| 1 — Core Foundation | 0.1.0 | **Done** (verified offline and in Studio) |
| 2 — Remote Security | 0.2.0 | **Done** (verified offline, in Studio, and over the real network) |
| 3 — Movement & Physics | 0.3.0 | **Done** (verified offline and live in Studio) |
| 4 — Combat Integrity | 0.4.0 | **Done** (verified offline and live in Studio) |
| 5 — Behavioural Intelligence | 0.5.0 | **Done** (verified offline; Studio run pending) |
| 6 — Risk Scoring & Evidence | 0.6.0 | **Done** (verified offline; Studio/DataStore run pending) |
| 7 — Hybrid Enforcement | 0.7.0 | **Done** (verified offline; Studio/live ban run pending) |
| 8 — Testing & Production Release | 1.0.0 | **Done**: release candidate 1.0.0-rc.1 on the canary channel; stable after the canary criteria (RELEASING.md) |

## Phase 1 — Core Foundation ✅

| Acceptance criterion | Verified by |
|----------------------|-------------|
| Framework initialises from validated configuration; invalid config fails with all errors | `Unit/Configuration`, `Integration/Framework` |
| Modules implement `Initialize/Start/Stop/Destroy`; definitions validated | `Unit/ModuleLoader` |
| Dependency ordering, missing/disabled/cyclic dependency handling | `Unit/ModuleLoader` |
| Explicit lifecycle state machine with published transitions | `Unit/ModuleLoader` |
| Error isolation: failing module, handler, task, sink | Unit + `ExploitSimulation/Pipeline` |
| Resource cleanup scoped to Start/Stop and Initialize/Destroy | `Unit/ModuleLoader`, `Integration/Framework` |
| Player sessions, rejoin, character generations, pre-existing players | `Integration/Framework` |
| Event bus with strict topics and recursion limit | `Unit/CorePrimitives` |
| Detection event schema, validation, sanitisation, identity binding | `Integration/Framework`, `ExploitSimulation/Pipeline` |
| Evidence infrastructure: bounded, merged, expiring, rejoin-safe | `Unit/EvidenceManager`, `ExploitSimulation/Pipeline` |
| Structured, rate-limited logging | `Unit/CorePrimitives` |
| Strict type check against Roblox definitions | `tools/check.sh` |

**Open item:** run `ServerStorage.ACFTests.Runner` inside Roblox Studio (see TESTING.md).
No Studio instance was connected when Phase 1 finished, so the suite has run under Lune
(Luau 0.10.5) but not yet in the Roblox VM.

## Phase 2 — Remote Security ✅

| Acceptance criterion | Verified by |
|----------------------|-------------|
| RemoteEvent, UnreliableRemoteEvent and RemoteFunction validation | `Integration/RemoteSecurity` |
| Argument type and structure validation with secure defaults and bounded traversal | `Unit/Validators`, `ExploitSimulation/Remotes` |
| Per-player, per-remote rate limiting and cooldowns | `Integration/RemoteSecurity`, `ExploitSimulation/Remotes` |
| Permission and ownership validation, fail-closed on errors | `Unit/Validators` (Policies), `Integration/RemoteSecurity` |
| Duplicate transaction protection with bounded memory | `Integration/RemoteSecurity`, `ExploitSimulation/Remotes` |
| Suspicious-pattern tracking; one invalid request is never reported | `ExploitSimulation/Remotes` (single, decay, laggy burst, sustained) |
| Reusable schemas usable by any game | `docs/REMOTE_SECURITY.md`; public `ACF.Validators`, `ACF.Permissions`, `ACF.Ownership` |
| Fail closed while stopped; handler errors isolated | `Integration/RemoteSecurity` |
| Measurable overhead | `tools/bench.sh` (~3 µs per accepted request under Lune) |

**Deviation:** `Shared/Protocol` moved to Phase 3. ACF creates no remotes of its own until
client telemetry exists, so a protocol module would have no consumer yet.

**Open item:** as for Phase 1, the Studio run of the suite is pending.

## Phase 3 — Movement & Physics ✅

| Acceptance criterion | Verified by |
|----------------------|-------------|
| Speed, flight, noclip, teleport, infinite jump, abnormal acceleration | `ExploitSimulation/Movement` |
| Unexpected velocity and physics manipulation (fling, extreme velocity, tp-walk mismatch) | `ExploitSimulation/Movement` (physics) |
| Latency, freezes and packet bunching | `Integration/MovementLegit` (1.5 s / 3 s freezes, jittery high ping), `Unit/MovementDetectors` (time credit) |
| Knockback, vehicles, custom mechanics, server teleports | `Integration/MovementLegit` (Impulse, seated vehicle, dash, AuthorizeTeleport) |
| Respawning and character replacement | `Integration/MovementLegit` (respawn far away; death ragdoll) |
| Moving platforms, climbing, swimming, falling, double-jump games | `Integration/MovementLegit` |
| Never punish a single position update | `ExploitSimulation/Movement` (single teleport not reported), `Unit/MovementDetectors` (ViolationTracker) |
| Configurable physical constraints | `ModuleSettings.Movement` / `.Physics`, per-detector enable flags |
| Measurable overhead | `tools/bench.sh` (~0.56 ms per tick for 50 players under Lune) |

Also delivered:

- `Shared/Protocol` and the optional `Telemetry` module with its client script (weak signal,
  off by default).
- The shared `Intelligence/ViolationTracker`. RemoteSecurity was refactored onto it, so
  detectors no longer duplicate suspicion logic.

**Network ownership.** Characters are client-owned, so every detector assumes the client
controls its own physics and judges only server-observed results. Explicit ownership
checks on world parts are not included; they are game-specific and expensive.

**Open items:**

- Run the Studio suite.
- Validate `RobloxPhysics` (the real probe) in Studio: R6/R15 clearance, seats, ladders,
  terrain water, platform `GroundSpeed`. This is the main Phase 3 risk, because the real
  probe has no offline test.

## Phase 4 — Combat Integrity ✅

| Acceptance criterion | Verified by |
|----------------------|-------------|
| Damage validation, calculated by the server | `Integration/CombatLegit` (falloff, headshots, DamageCalculator); `ExploitSimulation/Combat` (client-sent damage ignored) |
| Weapon and inventory ownership | `ExploitSimulation/Combat` (NotOwned, fabricated ids); providers fail closed |
| Fire rate and cooldowns | `ExploitSimulation/Combat` (rapid fire, cooldown bypass); `Integration/CombatLegit` (max-rate fire, bunched delivery, exact cooldown) |
| Attack distance and hit validation | `ExploitSimulation/Combat` (reach/kill aura, silent aim, spoofed origin, wall-bang) |
| Projectile validation | `ExploitSimulation/Combat` (instant hit, magic bullet, replay); `Integration/CombatLegit` (travel, gravity arc, pierce) |
| Ammunition | `ExploitSimulation/Combat` (infinite ammo, instant reload); `Integration/CombatLegit` (reload cycle, SetAmmo) |
| Ability validation | Ability kind with cooldown, range and optional target |
| Integration interfaces for game-defined rules | `API/IntegrationAPI` (DefineWeapon/Ability, providers); `examples/CombatWiring.server.luau` |
| No false positives on legitimate play | `Integration/CombatLegit` (16 scenarios, incl. high ping and moving targets) |
| Measurable overhead | `tools/bench.sh` (~16 µs per validated hit under Lune) |

Tests were checked for vacuity by mutation: disabling the origin check fails the
spoofed-origin exploit test, and zeroing the hit tolerance fails the laggy-shooter
legitimate test.

**Bug found and fixed during the phase:** projectile timing and trajectory originally
assumed a straight path to the impact. Every lobbed or angled projectile would have been
flagged `OffTrajectory`. Trajectories are now searched along the real path.

**Open items:**
- Studio validation of `RobloxPhysics.Describe`: accessory hits and NPC models.
- Running the full suite in Studio.

## Phase 5 — Behavioural Intelligence ✅

| Acceptance criterion | Verified by |
|----------------------|-------------|
| Per-player baselines with EMA and variance | `Unit/Statistics`, `Unit/BehavioralDetectors` (signal players) |
| CUSUM change detection over game-supplied signals | `ExploitSimulation/Behavioral` (10×, 4×, from-join, colluding); `Unit/BehavioralDetectors` (no single-bucket alarm) |
| Population baselines that the cheater cannot shift | Median baseline; colluding-exploiters test; accuracy excludes the player's own shots |
| Accuracy and headshot ratio with sample-size guards | `Unit/BehavioralDetectors` (short streaks, 80% player); `Integration/BehavioralLegit` (9,000 shots, lone strong player); `ExploitSimulation/Behavioral` (aimbot, head-lock) |
| Automation: low-variance timing, repeated invalid actions | `ExploitSimulation/Behavioral` (autoclickers, invalid floods, plus the documented limits); `Integration/BehavioralLegit` (human clicking, broke-clicking) |
| Each detector documents input, model, parameters, output and explanation | `docs/BEHAVIORAL.md`; module header comments; report `Details` |
| No false positives on legitimate play | `Integration/BehavioralLegit` (10 scenarios) |
| Tests are not vacuous | Six mutation spot checks, each caught (CHANGELOG 0.5.0) |
| Measurable overhead | `tools/bench.sh`: ~1.2 µs per `RecordSignal`, ~0.38 ms per 10 s tick for 50 players × 4 signals |

**Deviation:** the planned `Economy` module was folded into Behavioral signals. A rate
model over game-reported resource gains covers what it was meant to do.

**Bugs found in design, before shipping:**

- Counting only active buckets would have flagged players paid in lumps.
- `MinRatio` 2 would have flagged 2× gamepass owners (the mutation check confirms it).

Both fixes are in the false-positive suite.

**Open items:**
- Run the suite in Studio.
- Live check with real Combat shots from a client.

## Phase 6 — Risk Scoring and Evidence ✅

| Acceptance criterion | Verified by |
|----------------------|-------------|
| Weighted, decaying evidence model `R = min(100, Σ w·c·e^(−λt))` | `Integration/Risk` (formula to 4 decimal places), `Unit/RiskModel` |
| Confidence weighting | `Unit/RiskModel`; mutation: ignoring confidence fails 3 tests |
| Evidence expiration | `Integration/Risk` (decay lowers the band; departed users forgotten) |
| Duplicate detection suppression | `Integration/Risk` (burst counts once; 20 min of one cheat saturates in Restrict) |
| Correlation handling, no double counting | `Integration/Risk` (37.5 correlated versus 60 independent; Groups merge) |
| Session risk | `Integration/Risk` (survives rejoin) |
| Persistent risk where justified | `Integration/RiskPersistence` (only ≥ 40 saved, capped history, decays in days, failures fail safe, batching, retries, shutdown) |
| Evidence history | `GetRiskHistory`: detections, band changes, history loads, resets; bounded |
| Risk explanations | `Explanation` text and per-group `Kinds`; `Unit/RiskModel` |
| Response bands 0–19 / 20–39 / 40–59 / 60–79 / 80–100 | `Unit/RiskModel` (boundaries); configurable with validated ordering |
| Tests not vacuous | Eight mutation spot checks, each caught |
| Measurable overhead | `tools/bench.sh`: ~8 µs per detection (full pipeline); ~0.26 ms per recompute for 50 users |

**Design decision:** RiskEngine is a core service, not a module, so every module's
detections reach it and it cannot be Blocked by a module failure.

**Open items:**

- Run the suite in Studio.
- Exercise the real DataStore (`RobloxStore`) with API access enabled, including the
  `BindToClose` flush on a real shutdown.

## Phase 7 — Hybrid Enforcement ✅

| Acceptance criterion | Verified by |
|----------------------|-------------|
| Levels 0–7 | `Unit/EnforcementPolicy` (band mapping, escalation), `Integration/Enforcement` |
| Configurable policies | `Enforcement.*` (BandLevels, Corroboration, Bans, …); policy tests use configured values |
| Shadow mode | `Integration/Enforcement` (nothing applied, everything audited, shadow cases unapprovable) |
| Automatic action rejection | Tagged remotes and Combat refused; untagged allowed; not scored as suspicion |
| Controlled movement correction | Corrected to the last good position, with cooldown, MaxDistance and Movement tag required |
| Staff approval workflows | Cases: approve (proposed or chosen level), reject, review cases, expiry |
| Administrative overrides | Exempt and level overrides, expiry, removal levels refused |
| Ban expiration | Temporary bans enforced on join, then expire into history |
| Ban history | Record history: kicks, bans, unbans, expiries; bounded |
| Appeal references | `ACF-XXXXXXXX` in messages, records, audit and cases |
| Complete enforcement audit logs | Every decision and staff action; `By` required; bounded; published |
| Permanent bans need human approval by default | Policy and integration tests; mutation check |
| Detection, punishment and evidence separate | Enforcement is its own core service; modules only ask questions via `context.Enforcement` |
| Tests not vacuous | Seven mutation spot checks, each caught |

**Defaults chosen with care:** AutoBan is off. Permanent bans always need approval.
Statistical evidence never removes anyone automatically. A removed player is not removed
again without new evidence.

**Open items:**

- Live check of `Player:Kick`, `Players:BanAsync`/`UnbanAsync` and DataStore records in
  a published place.
- Run the suite in Studio.
- Movement correction with a real client (replication of the server PivotTo).

## Phase 8 — Testing and Production Release ✅ (release candidate)

| Requirement | Delivered by |
|-------------|--------------|
| Unit, integration and regression tests | 420 tests; regression register in TESTING.md |
| Adversarial simulations | `ExploitSimulation/*`; nine cheat types in `Simulation.luau` |
| Multiplayer load tests | 50-NPC Studio load (Phase 4); population simulation at 10/50/100 players. **Real multi-client load: canary period.** |
| Latency tests | `Integration/Latency` (to 0.9 s); live Network Simulator runs at 278 ms and 790 ms |
| Respawn tests | MovementLegit, morphs (Studio), respawns inside the simulation |
| DataStore failure tests | RiskPersistence, Enforcement (fake failures); live unpublished-place failures |
| Module failure tests | Pipeline, Framework; faulty module in the 2-hour soak |
| Detection accuracy | docs/EVALUATION.md: 90/90 cheat runs caught |
| False-positive rate | docs/EVALUATION.md: 0 over 50 honest player-hours |
| CPU overhead | docs/EVALUATION.md: ~1 ms per tick at 50 players; `tools/bench.sh` |
| Memory consumption | 2-hour churn soak: per-user structures bounded |
| Logging performance | ~0.6 µs per rate-limited Warn; ~0.17 µs per filtered Debug |
| Framework stability | Soak: faulty module isolated, others Running, no crashes |
| Development, canary and stable channels | `tools/release.sh`, RELEASING.md, `ACF.Channel` |

**Bugs Phase 8 found before release:**

- the uncapped time credit (a teleport bypass);
- the mid-jump lag false positive;
- four memory-retention issues.

The evaluation also drove a recalibration: RepeatCap, Speed severity and Behavioral
severity and confidence.

**Why release candidate, not stable:** two things need a published game with real
players, which an offline harness and single-client Studio cannot provide. They are
listed in the CHANGELOG under "Not verified".

## Later-phase notes carried from Phase 1

- Evidence records are per-server by design. Risk persists across servers as a summary
  (Phase 6); full evidence records do not.
- Behavioural evidence is statistical (confidence ≤ 0.8). Phase 7 never removes anyone on
  it alone (the corroboration rule).
- Behavioural population baselines are per server. Phase 6 persists risk, not baselines;
  cross-server baselines remain future work.
- Combat returns verdicts; applying damage stays with the game. Hit registration *rollback*
  (rewinding target positions by ping) is not implemented: tolerances scale with ping
  instead. Revisit if Phase 8 measurements show false refusals at high ping.
- Server-side movement correction (rubber-banding) is enforcement level 3, delivered in
  Phase 7.
- RemoteSecurity suspicion is per remote; the RiskEngine (Phase 6) now correlates across
  detectors.

```

`TESTING.md`:

```md
# Testing ACF

## Running

```bash
tools/test.sh               # all tests under Lune
tools/test.sh Evidence      # only tests whose full name contains "Evidence"
tools/check.sh              # strict static type check (luau-lsp + Roblox definitions)
```

**In Roblox Studio:** run `tools/build.sh`, open `build/ACF-Tests.rbxl` (or `rojo serve
test.project.json`), then in the command bar:

```lua
require(game:GetService("ServerStorage").ACFTests.Runner).run()
```

The same spec files run in both environments.

## How it works

`tests/lune/VirtualDataModel.luau` reads `test.project.json` and builds a virtual instance
tree. Its `require(instance)` executes ModuleScripts with `script`, `game` and `task`
provided, so ACF source runs unmodified. Platform services come from `tests/Specs/Fakes.luau`
through ACF's `Environment`:

- fake Players service and players;
- a manually advanced clock;
- a fake Heartbeat;
- deterministic ids;
- a capturing log sink.

Tests advance time with `fake.Clock.Advance(seconds)` and drive scheduled work with
`framework:Step()`. That makes time-dependent behaviour (rate limits, expiry, intervals)
deterministic.

## Suites

| Folder | Purpose | Current |
|--------|---------|---------|
| `Unit/` | One subsystem in isolation | CorePrimitives, Configuration, ModuleLoader, EvidenceManager, Validators (+ Policies), MovementDetectors (+ ViolationTracker), CombatRules, Statistics, BehavioralDetectors, RiskModel, EnforcementPolicy (+ BanRecords) |
| `Integration/` | Full framework with fakes | Framework (lifecycle, context, sessions, detection), ExampleDetector, RemoteSecurity, **MovementLegit** (19 legitimate-play scenarios that must produce no evidence), Telemetry, **CombatLegit** (16 legitimate-combat scenarios), CombatHarness (shared arena), **BehavioralLegit** (10 legitimate-behaviour scenarios), BehavioralHarness (N-player world, deterministic RNG), Risk (formula, decay, suppression, correlation, bands, history, bounds, end to end), RiskPersistence (shared fake Store across "servers": saving, seeding, caps, failures, latency races, batching, shutdown), Enforcement (restriction, correction, cases, automatic and approved removals, escalation, shadow/Off/Manual modes, bans across servers, storage and ban-API failures, overrides, audit) |
| `ExploitSimulation/` | Hostile inputs and misbehaving modules | Pipeline (floods, poisoned details, spoofing, rejoin reset); Remotes (type confusion, huge/deep tables, forged instances, replay, suspicion vs. honest lag); Movement (speed, teleport, flight, air jump, noclip, acceleration, fling, tp-walk); Combat (rapid fire, infinite ammo, unowned/fabricated weapons, reach, wall-bang, origin spoof, silent aim, cooldown bypass, instant/magic/replayed projectiles, dead shooter, malformed claims); Behavioral (currency exploits incl. colluding, aimbot, head-lock, autoclickers and their documented limits, invalid floods, hostile API input) |
| `Regression/` | One test per fixed bug, named after the bug | Created with the first reported bug |

Current result (v1.0.0): **420/420 passing** under Lune 0.10.5 and in Roblox Studio's VM
(0.748); type check clean.

**Phase 8 suites:**

- `Integration/Population` runs a 4-minute server with honest players and every cheat.
- `Integration/Latency` sweeps honest populations from 0.02 s to 0.9 s ping.
- `tools/evaluate.sh` runs the long evaluation; results are in `docs/EVALUATION.md`.
- The simulator is `tests/Specs/Simulation.luau`: seeded and deterministic, with clicks
  at 60 Hz frame times, not tick-aligned.

**False-positive policy.** Every movement, physics, combat or behavioural detector change
must keep `Integration/MovementLegit`, `Integration/CombatLegit` and
`Integration/BehavioralLegit` clean. A legitimate scenario that
produces evidence is a bug in the detector, not in the test.

**Mutation spot checks.** For each new detector, break its core check and confirm that an
exploit test fails. Then make its tolerance zero and confirm that a legitimate test fails.
That proves the suites are not vacuous.

- Phase 4 did this for origin and hit-tolerance.
- Phase 5 did it for the median baseline, the z clamp, the Wilson bound, the MinRatio
  guard, idle-bucket counting and the timing check.
- Phase 6 did it for correlation, saturation, duplicate suppression, confidence
  weighting, the stale-load guard, the history cap, the save threshold and save retries.
- Phase 7 did it for statistical corroboration, unapproved permanent bans, shadow mode,
  the new-evidence guard, the join ban check, exempt overrides and the temporary
  restriction.

**Enforcement fakes.** `Fakes.enforcer(players)` records `Kicks`, `Bans` and `Unbans`.
Kick makes the player leave, like Roblox does, and `FailBans` injects ban-API failures.
`Fakes.physics():Correct` records corrections.

**Storage fakes.** `Fakes.store()` is an in-memory `Environment.Store`:

- `Deferred = true` holds callbacks until `Complete()`, modelling DataStore latency;
- `FailLoads` / `FailSaves` inject failures;
- pass one store to several `Fakes.environment({ Store = s, ClockStart = t })` calls to
  simulate the same player on different servers at different times.

**Behavioural scenarios are deterministic.** `BehavioralHarness.rng(seed)` is a Park-Miller
generator, so a "random" honest population is identical on every run, in Lune and in Studio.

## Fakes and Roblox types

`Fakes.physics()` (included in `Fakes.environment()`) is a world of boxes with Roblox ray
semantics: a ray starting inside a part does not hit it. Character samples are scripted
with `fake.Physics:Set(character, fields)`, and ground clearance is computed from the boxes
below unless set explicitly.

`Fakes.instance`, `Fakes.vector3` and `Fakes.cframe` return **real** engine objects when
the suite runs in Studio. Under Lune they return tables, and the harness `typeof` reports
them as `Instance`/`Vector3`/`CFrame`. `Fakes.remoteEvent`/`remoteFunction` are always
fakes, because a real remote cannot be fired from the server. `Fakes.fire(remote, player,
...)` and `Fakes.invoke(...)` simulate client requests.

## Benchmarks

`tools/bench.sh` times hot paths under Lune. Use it to spot regressions, not to predict
Roblox server costs.

## Studio verification log

**2026-11-17**, Roblox Studio (scratch place, R15 character), v0.4.0:

**Full suite in Edit mode:** 278/278 passing in Roblox's own Luau VM. The fakes switch to
real `Instance`, `Vector3` and `CFrame` objects there.

**Live Play mode,** with ACF started via the real `RobloxEnvironment` (Players, Heartbeat,
RobloxPhysics):

| Area | Result |
|------|--------|
| Real probe on a standing R15 character | `GroundClearance` 0.004, JumpVelocity 53.15 (JumpHeight 7.2), State Running, WalkSpeed 16 |
| Idle character, and an authorized 250-stud server teleport | No detections |
| Three unauthorized 300-stud teleports | `TeleportDetected` reported (allowed 26 studs in 0.8 s) |
| Hovering 57 studs up (anchored root) | `Flight` reported after 2.6 s of airtime (allowed 1.54 s) |
| Movement sampler cost | 187 runs, 0 failures, ~0.08 ms per tick with 1 player |
| Combat with real raycasts and `Describe` against an NPC dummy | Body hit 25 and headshot 50 allowed; shot through a wall refused (`NoLineOfSight`); 190-stud shot refused (`OutOfRange`) |
| RemoteSecurity over the real network, fired from the Studio client | The valid request reached the handler. NaN, a 5,000-character string, a table instead of a string, and an extra argument were all rejected. A 30-request flood was rate-limited (29). Both patterns were reported as evidence. |

**Second session, same day** (test map built inside Play mode, so it vanished on Stop):

| Case | Result |
|------|--------|
| R6 and R15 NPC rigs on the baseplate | `GroundClearance` 0.000 for both. R6 HipHeight 0 is handled by the leg compensation; R15 has HipHeight 2.19. |
| Seat / vehicle | Seated via `Seat:Sit`, the seat moved 300 studs at 100 studs/s, then exited. No detections. |
| Moving platform | A conveyor carried the character at 41.6 studs/s (2.6× WalkSpeed). The probe read `GroundSpeed` = 40 on every sample. No detections. |
| Ladder | The client climbed a truss wall to y = 83 (Climbing state replicated), went over the top and fell 80 studs. No detections. |
| Terrain water | Swam in circles for 8 s. The server saw state `Swimming` and clearance 0 (water counts as ground). No detections. |
| Accessory hits | Hats resolve to the wearer's model. **Gap found:** hats counted as body shots. Fixed in `RobloxPhysics.Describe`: a Handle welded to the Head, or carrying a Hat/Hair/Face attachment, counts as head. Re-verified live: a proper AccessoryWeld hat and a WeldConstraint hat are head; a back accessory is not; a shot at the hat did 50 damage (2× headshot). |

**NPC load test, 2026-11-19** (ACF's own test place, Play mode). An isolated ACF instance
ran with a players adapter that presents 50 real R15 NPC rigs as players, plus the real
`RobloxPhysics`, raycasts and Heartbeat:

- 48 honest bots wandered and jumped across a 1024-stud map;
- 20 of them fired hitscan claims through Combat 4 times a second at the nearest bot;
- Bot1 teleported between two on-map points every 3 s;
- Bot2 hovered 40 studs up.

| Measure (60 s) | Result |
|----------------|--------|
| Movement + Physics sampling, 50 characters | 352 ticks, 0 failures, **1.43 ms average / 2.89 ms max per tick** (~0.7% of a core at 5 Hz) |
| Combat with real raycasts | 5,100 validated hits, all legitimate, all allowed; **~37 µs each** |
| Memory | Flat, 7.6–11.4 MB sawtooth over 60 s |
| Errors / warnings | 0 |
| Cheaters | Bot1 `TeleportDetected` and Bot2 `Flight`, reported continuously |
| Honest bots flagged | **0** |

A first attempt let random teleports carry Bot1 off the map, where it died after one jump;
ACF correctly didn't report a single teleport. Another bot fell under the floor while the
harness kept it shooting, and ACF correctly refused those shots as `NoLineOfSight`. Both
were harness bugs. The harness was then fixed to keep bots on the map.

**Morph test** (real player): the character was replaced four times by setting
`Player.Character`, with no allowance granted:

- R15 → R6 (280 studs away);
- → a 1.5×-scaled custom R15 (470 away);
- → R15 while walking (450 away);
- then a 70-stud walk.

`CharacterGeneration` incremented on every morph, `GroundClearance` was 0.00 for every rig,
and there were **zero detections**. Three unauthorized teleports on the morphed character
were then reported as `TeleportDetected`, so morphing does not blind ACF.

**Still requires real clients:** network-level load, meaning many clients' remote traffic,
client-owned physics and latency (Test → Clients and Servers, or a live server). Phase 8.

**Phase 8, 2026-11-20** (Studio 0.748, ACF's own test place, real `RobloxEnvironment`):

| Check | Result |
|-------|--------|
| Full suite in the Roblox VM | 420/420 in 2.7 s |
| DataStore in an unpublished place | Every call fails. Risk continues without history; enforcement retries 5×, then audits `PersistenceFailed`. No crash. |
| Movement correction, server-side teleport | 150-stud jumps pulled back to the anchor within 0.8 s; the client sees the corrected position |
| Movement correction, **client-side** teleport (as an exploit does it) | Pulled back after 0.25–0.28 s, 3/3. `TeleportDetected` evidence each time. |
| Idle-then-teleport (63 studs after ~60 s still) | **Not detected: bug found.** Fixed with `MaxTimeCreditSeconds`; regression test added. |
| Latency, Network Simulator 120±40 ms each way + 0.5 % loss | Measured NetworkPing 278 ms. 45 s of walking and 31 jumps: no evidence. |
| Latency, 350±120 ms each way | Measured 790 ms. 45 s, 24 jumps: no evidence. A client teleport at this ping was still detected (325 studs, 24 allowed). |
| `ACF:Kick` | `Player:Kick` → `PlayerRemoving` after 1.1 s; session ended; audit names the staff member |
| `ACF:Ban` → `Players:BanAsync` | Returns success instantly in an unpublished place (PlaceId 0) and does nothing; `GetBanHistoryAsync` 403. **Needs a published game to verify.** |

The Network Simulator settings were read before the test (all 0) and restored afterwards.

## Regression register

Every bug fixed after it was found, and the test that now guards it:

| Bug | Found by | Guarded by |
|-----|----------|------------|
| Noclip rays starting inside a part didn't hit it | Phase 3 | `ExploitSimulation/Movement` (Embedded mode) |
| Dash allowance expiry flagged the window that still held fast samples | Phase 3 | `MovementLegit` "a game dash…" |
| Knockback looked like a teleport | Phase 3 | `MovementLegit` "knockback…" |
| Moving platforms flagged as speed | Phase 3 | `MovementLegit` "riding a moving train…" |
| Projectiles assumed a straight path (lobs flagged `OffTrajectory`) | Phase 4 | `CombatLegit` gravity arc |
| Combat methods invisible to strict type checking | Phase 4 | `tools/check.sh` in `tools/verify.sh` |
| Hats counted as body shots | Phase 4, Studio | Studio check (`RobloxPhysics.Describe`), TESTING log 2026-11-17 |
| NPC limbs blocked their own ground ray | Phase 4, Studio | `RobloxPhysics.create({ GetCharacters })`, Studio NPC load test |
| Lump earners flagged (active-bucket rates) | Phase 5 design | `BehavioralLegit` "an honest economy…"; `Unit/BehavioralDetectors` |
| 2× gamepass owners flagged (MinRatio 2) | Phase 5 design | `BehavioralLegit`; mutation check |
| A kick's own detection cleared the new-evidence guard (re-kick on rejoin) | Phase 7 | `Enforcement` "…waits for new evidence after rejoin" |
| Offline ban records saved only on the next tick | Phase 7 | `Enforcement` "bans persist across servers…" |
| Bans waited for the stored record before removing the player | Phase 7 | `Enforcement` "a ban takes effect immediately…" |
| Lag spike mid-jump looked like flight | Phase 8 simulation | `MovementLegit` "lag: a 2.8 s freeze in mid-air…" |
| Spoofed velocity could hover forever under stall credit | Phase 8 design | `ExploitSimulation/Movement` "hovering while spoofing velocity…" |
| Uncapped time credit: idle then teleport | Phase 8 Studio | `ExploitSimulation/Movement` "standing still for a minute…" |
| Forgotten players re-created by decaying risk | Phase 8 soak | `Enforcement` "a forgotten player's decaying risk…" |
| Review cases opened while risk decayed or after leaving | Phase 8 soak | same test |
| Without a Store, pruning could drop the only copy of a ban | Phase 8 review | `Enforcement` "without a Store, ban records are never forgotten" |

New bugs get a test here first. Write one that fails, then fix the bug.

## What the offline harness cannot verify

Run these in Studio:

- Real `Players`/`RBXScriptSignal` behaviour: event ordering on join and leave, and
  `CharacterAdded` timing.
- `RunService.Heartbeat` cadence and `os.clock` precision on Roblox servers.
- `HttpService:GenerateGUID` and `DateTime` (used only by `RobloxEnvironment`).
- `Platform/RobloxPhysics`, the real character probe:
  - R6/R15 ground clearance;
  - `Humanoid:GetState()` replication for client-owned characters;
  - terrain water;
  - the moving-platform `GroundSpeed`;
  - ray cast exclusions.
- Real remote traffic: argument marshalling (metatables stripped, Instance references
  that don't replicate arrive as nil), `OnServerInvoke` behaviour and real client timing.
- Performance under real load (Phase 8: multiplayer load, latency and DataStore failure
  tests).

## Writing tests

```lua
-- tests/Specs/Unit/MyThing.spec.luau
local ACF = game:GetService("ServerScriptService").ACF
local Fakes = require(script.Parent.Parent.Fakes)

return function(t)
    t.describe("MyThing", function()
        t.it("does the thing", function()
            t.expect(1 + 1).toBe(2)
        end)
    end)
end
```

Files must end in `.spec.luau`. Matchers include `toBe`, `toEqual`, `toBeNil`, `toBeTruthy`,
`toBeFalsy`, `toBeGreaterThan`, `toBeLessThan`, `toBeCloseTo`, `toContain`, `toHaveLength`,
`toThrow` and `never.*`.

Never mark a test as passing without running it. If a test depends on something only
Studio provides, say so in the test name and in this file.

```

`docs/API.md`:

```md
# ACF Public API (v1.0.0)

```lua
local ACF = require(game:GetService("ServerScriptService").ACF)
```

Server only. Calling from a client errors ("ACF can only run on the server").

## Lifecycle

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:Initialize(config?)` | Validates configuration and builds core services. Raises with every problem listed; can be retried after fixing. Call once. |
| `ACF:RegisterModule(definition)` | Registers a detection module. Allowed before `Initialize` (queued) or before the first `Start`. Validates the definition and its `ModuleSettings` section. |
| `ACF:Start()` | Resolves dependencies, initialises and starts modules, then begins tracking players. Returns a status table. Raises if a `Critical` module fails. |
| `ACF:Stop()` | Ends sessions, stops modules, unbinds the scheduler. `Start` may be called again. |

## Remote security

See [REMOTE_SECURITY.md](REMOTE_SECURITY.md) for spec fields and validators.

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:ProtectEvent(remote, spec)` | Protects a RemoteEvent or UnreliableRemoteEvent. Requires `Initialize`. Returns `{ Name, Unprotect() }`. |
| `ACF:ProtectFunction(remote, spec)` | Protects a RemoteFunction. Rejected calls return `spec.RejectResponse`. |
| `ACF:FindUnprotectedRemotes(root)` | Remote instances under `root` without protection |
| `ACF:GetRemoteStats()` | `{ [name] = { Kind, Accepted, Rejected, HandlerErrors, Reports } }` |

## Combat (integration API)

See [COMBAT.md](COMBAT.md). Definitions and providers require `Initialize`; validation calls require ACF running.

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:DefineWeapon(id, definition)` / `ACF:DefineAbility(id, definition)` | Declare a weapon or ability and its rules |
| `ACF:SetCombatProvider(name, fn)` | `"Inventory"`, `"CanDamage"`, `"DamageCalculator"`; nil restores the default |
| `ACF:ValidateAttack(player, weaponId, claim)` | Hitscan / Melee / Ability verdict with server-computed damage |
| `ACF:FireProjectile(player, weaponId, claim)` / `ACF:ValidateProjectileHit(player, shotId, claim)` | Projectile fire and impact |
| `ACF:Reload(player, weaponId)`, `ACF:GetAmmo(...)`, `ACF:SetAmmo(...)` | Server-side ammo |
| `ACF:GetCombatStats()` | `{ Weapons = { [id] = { Shots, Hits, Rejected } }, Rejected }` |
| `ACF.CombatClaim` | Validator for claim arguments in `ProtectEvent` |

## Behaviour (integration API)

See [BEHAVIORAL.md](BEHAVIORAL.md). `DefineSignal` requires `Initialize`; recording requires ACF running.

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:DefineSignal(name, options?)` | Declare a per-player rate (currency, XP). `options`: `MaxPerSecond`, `Baseline = { Mean, StdDev }`, `MinStdDev`, `Statistical` |
| `ACF:RecordSignal(player, name, amount)` | Add a server-granted amount. Returns false for invalid amounts; errors for an undefined signal. |
| `ACF:RecordAction(player, action, valid?)` | Record a player-initiated action for timing and invalid-action checks |
| `ACF:GetBehaviorProfile(player)` | Per-player `{ Signals, Weapons, Actions }` statistics, or nil |
| `ACF:GetBehaviorStats()` | Baselines per signal, `CombatConnected`, `ShotsObserved`, violation counts |

## Risk

See [RISK.md](RISK.md). Accepts a Player, Session or UserId (departed players too).

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:GetRisk(player)` | `{ UserId, Risk (0-100), Band, Confidence, Persistent, Peak, Groups, Explanation }` |
| `ACF:GetRiskHistory(player)` | Detections that changed risk, band changes, loaded history, resets |
| `ACF:ResetRisk(player, { Reason?, Persistent? })` | Staff override; logged and recorded in history |
| `ACF:OnRiskChanged(handler)` | Fires when a player's band changes: `{ UserId, Session, Risk, Previous, Band, PreviousBand, Confidence, Explanation }` |

## Enforcement

See [ENFORCEMENT.md](ENFORCEMENT.md). Staff actions require `options.By` and are audited.

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:GetEnforcement(player)` | `{ Level, LevelName, Tags, RestrictedFor, Shadow, Override, OpenCase, Ban, History }` |
| `ACF:IsRestricted(player, tag, operation?)` | Whether operations tagged `tag` (a risk group, or `true` for any) are refused |
| `ACF:Kick(player, { By, Reason? })` | Returns the appeal reference |
| `ACF:Ban(player, { By, Reason?, Hours? })` | Temporary with `Hours`, otherwise permanent; works offline; returns the appeal reference |
| `ACF:Unban(player, { By, Reason? })` | Lifts the active ban |
| `ACF:SetEnforcementOverride(player, { Exempt? / Level?, Seconds?, By, Reason? })` / `ClearEnforcementOverride` | Admin overrides (levels 0–4) |
| `ACF:GetCases(filter?)`, `ACF:ApproveCase(id, { By, Level?, Hours?, Note? })`, `ACF:RejectCase(id, { By, Note? })` | Staff approval workflow |
| `ACF:GetAuditLog(filter?)` | Audit entries, oldest first; filter `{ UserId?, Type?, Since? }` |
| `ACF:OnEnforcementCase(handler)`, `ACF:OnEnforcementAudit(handler)` | Subscriptions |
| `ACF:LoadEnforcementRecord(player, callback)` | Stored ban and history |

Protected remotes accept `Restrict = "<tag>"` or `Restrict = true`; refusals use reason
`restricted`.

## Movement allowances

See [MOVEMENT.md](MOVEMENT.md). Both require ACF to be running with the Movement module active.

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:AuthorizeTeleport(player, seconds?)` | Call right before the server moves a character. Suppresses teleport/speed/noclip checks for `seconds` (default 1). |
| `ACF:AllowMovement(player, kind, seconds, options?)` | `kind` = `"Teleport"`, `"Speed"` (`options.Multiplier`, default 2), `"Flight"`, `"Noclip"`, `"Impulse"` or `"All"`. Returns false if the player has no session. |

## Queries

| Method | Returns |
|--------|---------|
| `ACF:GetStatus()` | `{ State, Version, Modules = { [name] = { State, Error?, Critical } }, ModuleOrder, Players, Evidence, Detections, EventBus, Scheduler, Logging }` |
| `ACF:GetSession(player)` | The active `Session` or `nil`. Accepts a Player, UserId or Session. |
| `ACF:GetEvidence(player, filter?)` | Frozen `EvidenceRecord`s, oldest first. Accepts a Player, Session or UserId; works after the player has left until expiry. `filter = { Detector?, Category?, Since? }` |
| `ACF:GetEvidenceSummary(player)` | `{ Records, TotalDetections, Merged, Evicted, Expired, ByCategory }` |
| `ACF:GetConfig(path)` | Resolved (frozen) configuration value, e.g. `"Evidence.RetentionSeconds"` |
| `ACF:GetRecentLogs(count?)` | Most recent log entries, oldest first |

## Events

| Method | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `ACF:OnDetection(handler)` | `handler({ Event, Record, IsNewRecord })` for every accepted detection. Returns a subscription with `:Disconnect()`. |
| `ACF:Subscribe(topic, handler)` | Subscribe to any core topic in `ACF.Topics` |

Handlers run synchronously and must not yield; use `task.spawn` for slow work. A handler
that throws 10 times in a row (configurable) is disconnected.

`ACF.Topics`: `FrameworkStateChanged`, `ModuleStateChanged`, `PlayerSessionStarted`,
`PlayerSessionEnded`, `CharacterSpawned`, `CharacterRemoving`, `DetectionReported`,
`RiskChanged`, `EnforcementAudit`, `EnforcementCase`.
See `src/Server/Core/Topics.luau` for payloads.

## Values

| Field | Description |
|-------|-------------|
| `ACF.Version` | `"1.0.0"` (release builds: e.g. `"1.0.0-rc.1"`) |
| `ACF.Channel` | `"development"`, `"canary"` or `"stable"` (RELEASING.md) |
| `ACF.Validators` | Argument validators for protected remotes |
| `ACF.Permissions`, `ACF.Ownership` | Reusable policy checks for protected remotes |
| `ACF.Schema` | Schema constructors for module `ConfigSchema`s |
| `ACF.RejectReasons` | `module_not_running`, `no_session`, `invalid_input`, `rate_limited` |
| `ACF.Framework` | The class, for isolated instances: `ACF.Framework.new(environment)` |

## Types

**DetectionEvent**
`{ Id, Detector, Category, Kind, UserId, SessionId, Severity, Confidence, Time, WallTime, Details }`

**EvidenceRecord**
`{ Id, UserId, SessionId, Detector, Category, Kind, Severity, Confidence, Count, FirstSeen, LastSeen, FirstWallTime, LastWallTime, FirstEventId, Details }`

**Session**
`{ SessionId, UserId, Name, Player, JoinedAt, JoinedWallTime, Character, CharacterGeneration, Active, Cleanup }`
plus `:GetState(key, factory?)` and `:ClearState(key)`.

`Time`, `FirstSeen`, `LastSeen` and `JoinedAt` are monotonic seconds (`os.clock`).
The `*WallTime` fields are Unix seconds.

```

`docs/BEHAVIORAL.md`:

```md
# Behavioural Intelligence (v1.0.0)

`Behavioral` is a built-in module, enabled by default. It finds cheating that no single
request reveals, only a pattern over time:

- income far above everyone else's;
- aim that is too accurate for too long;
- clicks spaced like a machine's;
- a client that keeps sending actions its own UI can't produce.

It only sees data the server already trusts:

- amounts the game's server code grants;
- actions the game's server code records;
- shots that Combat has already validated.

Nothing is read from the client.

| Detector | Data source | Reported Kind | Default severity |
|----------|-------------|---------------|------------------|
| Signal hard limit | `RecordSignal` | `SignalRateLimit` | 30 |
| Signal anomaly | `RecordSignal` | `SignalAnomaly` | 30 |
| Accuracy | Combat (hitscan and projectile) | `AccuracyAnomaly` | 30 |
| Headshots | Combat (hitscan and projectile) | `HeadshotAnomaly` | 30 |
| Timing | `RecordAction` | `RegularTiming` | 15 |
| Invalid actions | `RecordAction(…, false)` | `RepeatedInvalidActions` | 10 |

**Behavioural evidence is statistical.** Confidence is capped at 0.8
(`Violation.MaxConfidence`), and every report carries the numbers that produced it. Treat
it as a reason to look closer, not as proof. Phase 7 enforcement will not act on
behavioural evidence alone.

## Quick start

```lua
local ACF = require(game.ServerScriptService.ACF)
ACF:Initialize({})
ACF:DefineSignal("Coins")                          -- statistical only
ACF:DefineSignal("Gems", { MaxPerSecond = 2 })     -- plus a hard limit
ACF:Start()

-- Wherever the SERVER grants the resource:
local function giveCoins(player, amount)
    data[player].Coins += amount
    ACF:RecordSignal(player, "Coins", amount)
end

-- Wherever the server handles a player-initiated action:
clickRemote.OnServerEvent:Connect(function(player)
    ACF:RecordAction(player, "Click")
end)
buyRemote.OnServerEvent:Connect(function(player, itemId)
    local item = Shop[itemId]
    -- This game's client only shows items the player can afford, so an unknown item or
    -- an unaffordable purchase never comes from the real client (see "Invalid actions").
    local valid = item ~= nil and data[player].Coins >= item.Price
    ACF:RecordAction(player, "Buy", valid)
    if valid then
        purchase(player, item)
    end
end)
```

Combat accuracy needs no code. If you use `ACF:ValidateAttack` / `FireProjectile`, the
statistics come from Combat automatically.

## Signals: resource and progression rates

### Model

1. **Buckets.** Each player's amounts are summed over buckets of `BucketSeconds` (10 s)
   and turned into a rate per second.
   - A player's first, partial bucket is discarded.
   - After that, *every* bucket counts, empty ones included. A player paid in lumps (100
     coins every 20 s) and one paid steadily (5 per second) then have the same mean.
2. **Personal baseline.** Each player keeps an EWMA mean and variance of their own rate
   (`SelfAlpha` 0.2, roughly the last 10 buckets).
3. **Population baseline.** This is robust:
   - the **median** of eligible players' means;
   - scale = the largest of the median personal standard deviation, 1.4826 × the median
     absolute deviation of the means, `MinStdDev`, and `MinRelativeStdDev` × median.

   A player is eligible after `MinPlayerBuckets` buckets and with activity in the last
   `ActiveWithinBuckets` buckets, so AFK players don't drag the baseline down. At least
   `MinContributors` (4) eligible players are needed.

   **One cheater cannot move a median.** The model only assumes most players in the
   server are honest. Two exploiters among seven are both caught (tested).
4. **Change detection.** Each bucket of a recently active player is standardised against
   the baseline (z, clamped to ±`ZClamp` = 3) and fed into an upper CUSUM:
   `S ← max(0, S + z − k)`, with k = 0.5. It alarms when S > h, with h = 6.
   - Empty or normal buckets pull S back down.
   - With the clamp, **an alarm needs at least three strongly anomalous buckets**. No
     single payout, however large, can raise one.
5. **Practical significance.** An alarm reports only when the player's own mean is at
   least `MinRatio` (3) × the baseline.
   - That guard keeps 2× coin gamepass owners clean; the tests show they are otherwise
     flagged.
   - If your multipliers can exceed 3×, record the amount *before* multipliers, or raise
     `MinRatio`.

`SignalAnomaly` details include:

| Field | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `Signal` | Which signal alarmed |
| `RatePerSecond` | Rate in the bucket that tipped it |
| `PlayerMean` | The player's own mean rate |
| `BaselineMean`, `BaselineScale` | The baseline it was compared with |
| `BaselineSource` | `Population` or `Defined` |
| `BaselinePlayers` | How many players formed the baseline |
| `Ratio` | `PlayerMean / BaselineMean` |
| `Cusum` | The CUSUM statistic when it alarmed |
| `Buckets` | How many buckets the run lasted |

### Small servers

With fewer than 4 active players there is no population baseline. **Statistical signal
detection is then off** (tested). Two ways to cover small servers:

```lua
-- 1. A known expected rate (per second, idle time included) from your own analytics:
ACF:DefineSignal("Coins", { Baseline = { Mean = 5, StdDev = 2 } })
-- 2. A hard limit on what is physically possible:
ACF:DefineSignal("Coins", { MaxPerSecond = 100 })
```

### Hard limit (`MaxPerSecond`)

`SignalRateLimit` is reported when a bucket's rate exceeds `MaxPerSecond` × 1.1. Its weight
is 1–2 by excess, so it needs at least two buckets. Set it to what is *impossible* over 10
seconds, not to what is merely unusual. `Statistical = false` turns off the CUSUM for a
signal that should only have a hard limit.

### Signal options

| Option | Default | Meaning |
|--------|---------|---------|
| `MaxPerSecond` | none | Hard limit |
| `Baseline` | none | `{ Mean, StdDev }` per second. Replaces the population baseline. |
| `MinStdDev` | 0 | Floor on the baseline scale, in signal units per second |
| `Statistical` | true | Run the CUSUM |

## Combat accuracy

Combat publishes every accepted shot on the `Combat.Shot` topic. Hits are counted only
when they land on a character and pass all of Combat's checks. Each projectile counts its
first character hit, not pierce hits. Melee and abilities are excluded.

**Model.** Per player and per weapon, ACF keeps decayed counts (half-life 600 s) of shots,
hits and headshots, plus the same totals for the server. Every `EvaluateIntervalSeconds`
(10 s) it computes:

- the **Wilson lower bound** (z = 3.09, one-sided 99.9%) of the player's hit ratio;
- the server's ratio for that weapon **with the player's own shots removed**.

| Check | Minimum sample | Reported when the lower bound exceeds |
|-------|----------------|---------------------------------------|
| Accuracy | 40 shots | max(server ratio + 0.35, **0.90**) |
| Headshots per hit | 20 hits | max(server ratio + 0.40, **0.75**) |

When the server has fewer than 200 shots (or 100 hits) on that weapon, only an absolute
floor applies: **0.95** for accuracy and **0.90** for headshots.

The lower bound is the skill guard. A short perfect streak has a wide interval and is not
flagged. Each violation weighs 1, and the report threshold is 3, so the bound must stay
above the threshold for three evaluations.

What the tests established:

| Player | Result |
|--------|--------|
| Shooters at 30–80% accuracy, up to 55% headshots, 9,000 validated shots | Clean |
| A lone player at 85% accuracy and 50% headshots | Clean |
| 99% accuracy | Reported |
| 95% headshots at normal accuracy | Reported (headshots only) |

Details include `Weapon`, `Shots`/`Hits`/`Headshots`, `Ratio`, `LowerBound`, `Threshold`,
`PopulationRatio` and `Basis` (`Population` or `Absolute`).

**Tune per game.** In a game where 90% hitscan accuracy is normal (huge hitboxes, slow
targets), raise `AccuracyFloor`.

## Actions: timing and invalid actions

### Timing (`RegularTiming`)

Humans are irregular. Even deliberately rhythmic tapping varies by several percent from
one tap to the next.

- **Model.** The intervals between one player's actions of one kind are judged over
  non-overlapping windows of 30. A window is reported when its coefficient of variation
  (std ÷ mean) is below `MaxCV` (0.02) and its mean interval is between 0.1 s and 10 s.
- A gap over 30 s starts a new run.
- Each window weighs 1.5, so two windows are needed.

**Only record actions the player initiates.** Anything the server triggers on a timer
(auto-collect, regeneration, a tick reward) is perfectly regular by construction and
*will* be reported.

**Known limitation.** The server sees each action when it arrives, on a 60 Hz frame and
after network jitter. That adds spread the clicker never had:

| Autoclicker | Result (tested) |
|-------------|-----------------|
| 1 s, any frame phase | Reported |
| 0.5 s, mid-frame, 3 ms jitter | Reported |
| 0.5 s exactly on a frame boundary | Missed: jitter flips each click between two frames, adding ±17 ms |
| 0.25 s with 15 ms jitter | Missed |

Clickers that add random delays defeat this detector entirely. It catches simple macros;
nothing more is claimed.

### Invalid actions (`RepeatedInvalidActions`)

`RecordAction(player, action, false)` marks an action as invalid.

- **Model.** Decayed counts (half-life 60 s). The report needs at least 20 decayed invalid
  actions, and a Wilson lower bound (z = 2.33) of the invalid ratio above 0.8, for three
  evaluations.
- A frustrated player clicking "Buy" while broke is not reported (tested). A script firing
  a purchase remote three times a second for a minute is.

**Only mark an action invalid when your own client could never send it.** For example:

- the client hides the button when the player can't afford the item;
- the target is out of range in the client's own UI.

If your UI allows the action and the server refuses it, that's normal play: record it as
valid.

## API

| Method | Returns | Notes |
|--------|---------|-------|
| `ACF:DefineSignal(name, options?)` | — | After `Initialize`. Names are 1–64 characters and unique; at most 64 signals. Unknown option keys error. |
| `ACF:RecordSignal(player, name, amount)` | boolean | `amount` must be finite, ≥ 0 and ≤ 1e12. Otherwise it returns `false` and changes nothing. An undefined signal errors. |
| `ACF:RecordAction(player, action, valid?)` | boolean | At most 32 distinct actions per player; further names are dropped (`ActionsDropped`). |
| `ACF:GetBehaviorProfile(player)` | table? | `Signals`, `Weapons`, `Actions`: per-player statistics |
| `ACF:GetBehaviorStats()` | table | Baselines per signal, `CombatConnected`, `ShotsObserved`, violation counts |

All state is per session: leaving the server clears it. Population baselines are
per server.

## Configuration (`ModuleSettings.Behavioral`)

| Section | Key | Default |
|---------|-----|---------|
| `Signals` | BucketSeconds | 10 |
| | SelfAlpha | 0.2 |
| | MinPlayerBuckets | 3 |
| | ActiveWithinBuckets | 6 |
| | MinContributors | 4 |
| | MinRelativeStdDev | 0.1 |
| | ZClamp | 3 |
| | CusumK | 0.5 |
| | CusumH | 6 |
| | MinRatio | 3 |
| | HardLimitTolerance | 0.1 |
| | MaxSignals | 64 |
| `Accuracy` | HalfLifeSeconds | 600 |
| | EvaluateIntervalSeconds | 10 |
| | ConfidenceZ | 3.09 |
| | MinShots | 40 |
| | AccuracyMargin | 0.35 |
| | AccuracyFloor | 0.9 |
| | AccuracyFloorWithoutBaseline | 0.95 |
| | MinPopulationShots | 200 |
| | MinHits | 20 |
| | HeadshotMargin | 0.4 |
| | HeadshotFloor | 0.75 |
| | HeadshotFloorWithoutBaseline | 0.9 |
| | MinPopulationHits | 100 |
| `Actions` | WindowSize | 30 |
| | MaxCV | 0.02 |
| | MinMeanIntervalSeconds | 0.1 |
| | MaxMeanIntervalSeconds | 10 |
| | MaxGapSeconds | 30 |
| | InvalidHalfLifeSeconds | 60 |
| | MinInvalid | 20 |
| | MaxInvalidRatio | 0.8 |
| | InvalidConfidenceZ | 2.33 |
| | MaxActionsPerPlayer | 32 |
| `Detectors` | SignalRateLimit, SignalAnomaly, Accuracy, Headshots, Timing, InvalidActions | all true |
| `Severity` | SignalRateLimit | 30 |
| | SignalAnomaly | 30 |
| | Accuracy | 30 |
| | Headshots | 30 |
| | Timing | 15 |
| | InvalidActions | 10 |
| `Violation` | HalfLifeSeconds | 120 |
| | ReportThreshold | 3 |
| | ReportIntervalSeconds | 60 |
| | ConfidenceScale | 3 |
| | MaxConfidence | 0.8 |

## Performance

Measured with `tools/bench.sh` under Lune:

| Operation | Cost |
|-----------|------|
| `RecordSignal` | ~1.2 µs |
| `RecordAction` | ~1.9 µs |
| Bucket close and evaluation, 50 players × 4 signals, every 10 s | ~0.38 ms |

## Assumptions and limits

- Most players in a server are honest; the median baseline fails if they are not.
- Population baselines are per server and reset when it shuts down. Cross-server baselines
  need persistence (Phase 6).
- Statistical detectors catch *sustained* abuse. A single large exploit followed by
  normal play is a job for the hard limit, or for your own server-side validation.
- Accuracy depends on the game's hit model. Defaults are conservative; tune the floors per
  game.

```

`docs/COMBAT.md`:

```md
# Combat Integrity (v0.5.0)

`Combat` is a built-in module, enabled by default. It is passive until you define weapons.

The server decides every hit and every point of damage. The client sends a **claim**:
"I fired weapon X from here, and hit this part at this point". ACF checks the claim
against server-observed state and returns a verdict, with damage it computed itself. The
client can never supply damage, fire rate, range or ammo.

## Quick start

```lua
ACF:Initialize({})
ACF:DefineWeapon("Rifle", {
    Kind = "Hitscan", Damage = 25, HeadshotMultiplier = 2, FireRate = 10, MaxRange = 400,
    Falloff = { Start = 150, End = 400, MinMultiplier = 0.6 },
    Ammo = { Magazine = 30, Reserve = 120, ReloadSeconds = 2 },
})
ACF:ProtectEvent(remotes.Fire, {
    Arguments = { ACF.Validators.string({ MaxLength = 32 }), ACF.CombatClaim },
    Handler = function(player, weaponId, claim)
        local result = ACF:ValidateAttack(player, weaponId, claim)
        if result.Allowed and result.Target and result.Target.Humanoid then
            result.Target.Humanoid:TakeDamage(result.Damage)
        end
    end,
})
ACF:Start()
```

A complete version is in `examples/CombatWiring.server.luau`.

## Definitions (`ACF:DefineWeapon` / `ACF:DefineAbility`)

| Field | Default | Notes |
|-------|---------|-------|
| `Kind` | required | `"Hitscan"`, `"Projectile"`, `"Melee"` or `"Ability"` (`DefineAbility` sets it) |
| `Damage` | 0 | Base damage per hit |
| `HeadshotMultiplier` | 1 | Applied when the hit part is the `Head`, or a head-worn accessory (a hat or hair Handle welded to the head, or with a Hat/Hair/Face attachment) |
| `Falloff` | none | `{ Start, End, MinMultiplier }`: linear from 1 at Start to MinMultiplier at End |
| `FireRate` | required for Hitscan/Projectile | Shots per second |
| `Burst` | 1 | Shots allowed back-to-back above the rate (burst weapons) |
| `Cooldown` | 0 | Seconds between uses (abilities) |
| `MaxRange` | required | Studs |
| `Ammo` | infinite | `{ Magazine, Reserve = math.huge, ReloadSeconds = 0 }` |
| `ProjectileSpeed` | required for Projectile | Studs/s |
| `ProjectileGravity` | 0 | Downward acceleration, studs/s² |
| `ProjectileLifetime` | 5 | Seconds |
| `Pierce` | 0 | Extra targets one projectile may hit |
| `RequireLineOfSight` | true | |
| `RequireTarget` | true for Melee | Abilities may target a point (`HitPosition`) instead |

Invalid definitions raise at definition time with the offending field. Unknown fields are
rejected.

## Calls

| Method | Use |
|--------|-----|
| `ACF:ValidateAttack(player, weaponId, claim)` | Hitscan, Melee and Ability. Claim: `{ Origin?, Target?, HitPosition? }` |
| `ACF:FireProjectile(player, weaponId, claim)` | When a projectile is fired. Claim: `{ Origin, Direction, ShotId }` |
| `ACF:ValidateProjectileHit(player, shotId, claim)` | When it lands. Claim: `{ Target?, HitPosition? }` |
| `ACF:Reload(player, weaponId)` | Server-timed reload; returns `(ok, reason?)` |
| `ACF:GetAmmo(player, weaponId)` | `magazine, reserve, reloading` (server truth) |
| `ACF:SetAmmo(player, weaponId, magazine, reserve?)` | Pickups, loadouts, game-managed ammo |
| `ACF:SetCombatProvider(name, fn)` | See providers below |
| `ACF.CombatClaim` | Remote argument validator for claims |

**Result:**

```
{ Allowed, Reason?, ShotAccepted, Damage, Headshot, Target?, Distance? }
```

- `Target` is `{ X, Y, Z, Model, Humanoid, Alive, Player, IsHead }`.
- `ShotAccepted = true, Allowed = false` means the shot was legitimate (ammo consumed) but
  the hit was refused.
- Apply `Damage` yourself; ACF never changes game state.

## Providers

| Provider | Signature | Default |
|----------|-----------|---------|
| `Inventory` | `(player, weaponId, definition) -> boolean` | A Tool named `weaponId` in the character or Backpack. Abilities are always allowed. |
| `CanDamage` | `(attacker, targetInfo) -> boolean` | No damage to a teammate on the same non-neutral Team |
| `DamageCalculator` | `(info) -> number` | None. `info` = `{ Attacker, Weapon, Target, Distance, Headshot, BaseDamage }` |

A provider that errors **fails closed**: it is treated as a denial and logged. A
`DamageCalculator` that errors falls back to the base damage.

## Checks

**Stage 1: was the shot legitimate?** A passing shot consumes ammo and starts the cooldown.

| Reason | Check | Weight |
|--------|-------|--------|
| `UnknownWeapon` | Weapon id not defined (creates no state) | 3 |
| `InvalidClaim` | Malformed claim (wrong types, NaN, missing Origin/Direction/ShotId) | 2 |
| `ShooterDead` | No character or Health ≤ 0 | 1 |
| `NotOwned` | Inventory provider denies | 3 |
| `Reloading` / `NoAmmo` | Server ammo state | 1 / 1 |
| `Cooldown` | Within Cooldown (−0.1 s tolerance) | 1 |
| `OriginMismatch` | Claimed origin > 8 studs (+ ping × speed) from the shooter's server position | 2 |
| `FireRate` | Token bucket: Burst capacity, refill FireRate × 1.15 | 1.5 |

**Stage 2: is the hit plausible?** The hit is refused, but the shot still counts.

| Reason | Check | Weight |
|--------|-------|--------|
| `HitMismatch` | Claimed hit point > 6 studs (+ ping × 32) from the part's server position | 1 |
| `SelfHit` | Target is the shooter's own character | 0.5 |
| `TargetDead` | Target already dead (kill races are normal) | **0** |
| `OutOfRange` | Beyond MaxRange × 1.1 + 5 (+ ping × 32) | 2 |
| `FriendlyFire` | CanDamage denies | 0.5 |
| `NoLineOfSight` | World geometry between origin and hit (characters ignored) | 1.5 |
| `UnknownShot` | Projectile hit for a shot never fired, or already consumed | 2 |
| `DuplicateShot` | ShotId reused while still in flight | 2 |
| `ProjectileExpired` | Impact after ProjectileLifetime | 1 |
| `ProjectileTiming` | Impact earlier than the projectile could arrive along its path (+ ping, + 0.15 s) | 2 |
| `OffTrajectory` | The fired path never passes within 6 studs + 3% of distance of the impact | 2 |

**Projectile paths are searched, not assumed straight.** ACF finds the earliest moment the
real trajectory (direction, speed, gravity) passes within tolerance of the impact. Lobbed
and angled shots are therefore judged correctly, and the timing check uses that moment.

**Reporting.** Rejections feed the shared `ViolationTracker` (half-life 20 s, threshold 6),
so one desynced shot never reports. Reported kinds are `Combat<Reason>`, e.g.
`CombatFireRate` or `CombatNoLineOfSight`. Weights, severities and tolerances live under
`ModuleSettings.Combat`.

**Accuracy and headshot ratios** are judged by the Behavioral module, statistically and
against the server's own players ([BEHAVIORAL.md](BEHAVIORAL.md#combat-accuracy)). Combat
feeds it through the `Combat.Shot` topic:

- one `{ Event = "Shot", Hit, Headshot }` per accepted shot;
- one `{ Event = "Hit" }` for a projectile's first character hit.

Reaction time is not judged: the server cannot observe it reliably.

## Latency

| Allowance | Default |
|-----------|---------|
| Ping compensation | Up to 0.5 s, widening the range, hit-point and origin tolerances |
| Origin tolerance | Includes ping × the shooter's allowed speed |
| Fire rate | 15% refill margin, so jittery delivery isn't penalised |

All three are configurable. Both the legitimate-combat and cheat suites run with these
defaults.

## Configuration (`ModuleSettings.Combat`)

| Key | Default |
|-----|---------|
| OriginToleranceStuds | 8 |
| RangeToleranceFraction / RangeToleranceStuds | 0.1 / 5 |
| HitToleranceStuds | 6 |
| MaxTargetSpeed | 32 |
| MaxPingCompensationSeconds | 0.5 |
| LineOfSightEndToleranceStuds | 2 |
| CooldownToleranceSeconds | 0.1 |
| FireRateTolerance | 1.15 |
| ProjectileTimingToleranceSeconds | 0.15 |
| ProjectileTrajectoryToleranceStuds / Fraction | 6 / 0.03 |
| MaxPendingProjectiles | 32 per player |
| Weights.<Reason>, Severity.<Reason> | See tables above |
| Violation | HalfLifeSeconds 20, ReportThreshold 6, ReportIntervalSeconds 10, ConfidenceScale 10 |

## Performance (Lune, indicative)

| Case | Cost |
|------|------|
| Validated hitscan hit (with line-of-sight cast) | ~16 µs |
| Projectile fire + arcing hit validation | ~36 µs |

`Physics:Describe` (the real target lookup in `RobloxPhysics`) still needs a Studio run.

`docs/CONFIGURATION.md`:

```md
# ACF Configuration Reference (v1.0.0)

Pass overrides to `ACF:Initialize`. Unspecified keys use the defaults below. Unknown keys,
wrong types and out-of-range values are rejected. Every problem is listed at once, with
suggestions for typos. Resolved configuration is frozen.

| Key | Default | Range / values | Notes |
|-----|---------|----------------|-------|
| `Debug` | `false` | boolean | Forces log level Debug |
| `LogLevel` | `"Info"` | Debug, Info, Warn, Error | |
| `Logging.BufferSize` | 500 | 16–10000 (int) | Entries kept for `GetRecentLogs` |
| `Logging.RateLimitBurst` | 20 | 1–1000 (int) | Identical messages allowed per window |
| `Logging.RateLimitWindowSeconds` | 10 | 0.1–3600 | |
| `Enforcement.Mode` | `"Hybrid"` | Hybrid, Manual, Off | Automatic levels 0–4; removals automatic only when corroborated (Hybrid) |
| `Enforcement.AutoKick` | `true` | boolean | Automatic kicks on corroborated evidence (Hybrid) |
| `Enforcement.AutoBan` | `false` | boolean | Automatic temporary bans; permanent bans still require approval by default |
| `Enforcement.ShadowMode` | `false` | boolean | Decide and audit, apply nothing automatic. Recommended first. |
| `Modules` | `{}` | `{ [name] = boolean }` | Enables/disables modules by name; unlisted modules use their `EnabledByDefault` |
| `ModuleSettings` | `{}` | `{ [name] = table }` | Validated against each module's `ConfigSchema` at registration; keys with no matching module fail at `Start` |
| `Detection.ReportsPerSecond` | 10 | 0.1–1000 | Sustained reports per (player, detector) |
| `Detection.ReportBurst` | 30 | 1–10000 (int) | Burst above the sustained rate |
| `Detection.MaxDetailKeys` | 32 | 0–256 (int) | Per Details table |
| `Detection.MaxDetailStringLength` | 256 | 8–4096 (int) | Longer strings are truncated |
| `Evidence.MaxRecordsPerPlayer` | 64 | 1–1024 (int) | Least-recently-seen eviction |
| `Evidence.RetentionSeconds` | 1800 | 1–86400 | Records unseen this long are pruned |
| `Evidence.DuplicateWindowSeconds` | 5 | 0–3600 | Same (Detector, Kind) within the window merges |
| `Evidence.PruneIntervalSeconds` | 30 | 1–3600 | |
| `Scheduler.MaxConsecutiveFailures` | 5 | 1–1000 (int) | Then the task is disabled |
| `EventBus.MaxHandlerFailures` | 10 | 1–1000 (int) | Then the handler is disconnected |
| `EventBus.MaxDispatchDepth` | 8 | 1–64 (int) | Nested publish limit |

**`Enforcement`** configures graduated enforcement: mode, shadow mode, band levels,
corroboration, correction, bans, cases, audit and messages. All keys are in
[ENFORCEMENT.md](ENFORCEMENT.md#configuration-enforcement).

**`Risk`** configures the central risk engine: half-life, repeat cap, correlation groups,
bands and opt-in persistence. All keys are in [RISK.md](RISK.md#configuration-risk).

**Built-in modules.** Each has its settings under `ModuleSettings.<Name>`:

| Module | Default | Settings reference |
|--------|---------|--------------------|
| `RemoteSecurity` | on | [REMOTE_SECURITY.md](REMOTE_SECURITY.md#configuration-modulesettingsremotesecurity) |
| `Movement` | on | [MOVEMENT.md](MOVEMENT.md#configuration) |
| `Physics` | on (requires Movement) | [MOVEMENT.md](MOVEMENT.md#configuration) |
| `Combat` | on (passive until weapons are defined) | [COMBAT.md](COMBAT.md#configuration-modulesettingscombat) |
| `Behavioral` | on (accuracy from Combat; signals and actions once the game records them) | [BEHAVIORAL.md](BEHAVIORAL.md#configuration-modulesettingsbehavioral) |
| `Telemetry` | off | [MOVEMENT.md](MOVEMENT.md#telemetry-optional-weak-signal) |

There is no separate `Economy` module: resource-gain detection is provided by
Behavioral signals (`ACF:DefineSignal`). Configuring `Modules.Economy` logs "Configured
module is not registered".

## Module schemas

```lua
ConfigSchema = ACF.Schema.Table({
    Threshold = ACF.Schema.Number(10, { Min = 0, Max = 100, Integer = false }),
    Enabled   = ACF.Schema.Boolean(true),
    Label     = ACF.Schema.String("x", { MaxLength = 32 }),
    Mode      = ACF.Schema.Enum({ "Strict", "Lenient" }, "Lenient"),
    Weights   = ACF.Schema.Map(ACF.Schema.Number(1, { Min = 0 }), { Default = 1 }),
})
```

Schema defaults are themselves validated when the schema is built.
